//! Table names may change; numeric row, reference and receipt lifetimes do not.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#8-wave-21-release-evolution — renames are identity moves, never row copies or merges.
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
pub(super) const VERSION:&str="snapback4-release-store-9";
fn refuse(s:impl Into<String>)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Structural{code:"E_ADOPT",message:s.into()}}

// Only structural table identifiers change. Literal values, column names,
// operation names and authored defaults are never table identifiers.
fn identifiers(value:&mut serde_json::Value,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>) {
    match value {
        serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for x in xs {identifiers(x,map)},
        serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>for (key,x) in fields {
            if key=="Literal" || key=="defaults" {continue;}
            if matches!(key.as_str(),"table"|"parent") {if let Some(name)=x.as_str().and_then(|s|map.get(s)){*x=serde_json::Value::String(name.clone());continue;}}
            identifiers(x,map);
        },_=>()
    }
}
fn mapped(name:&str,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->String {map.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||name.into())}
fn remap<T:serde::Serialize+serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(value:&T,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<T,RuntimeError> {
    let mut json=serde_json::to_value(value).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;identifiers(&mut json,map);
    serde_json::from_value(json).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))
}
pub(crate) fn schema(mut schema:Schema,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<Schema,RuntimeError> {
    if map.is_empty(){return Ok(schema)}
    schema.tables=std::mem::take(&mut schema.tables).into_iter().map(|(name,table)|Ok((mapped(&name,map),remap(&table,map)?))).collect::<Result<_,RuntimeError>>()?;
    for m in &mut schema.maintains {*m=remap(m,map)?;m.source=mapped(&m.source,map);m.target=mapped(&m.target,map);}
    for f in &mut schema.backfills {fill(f,map,false)?;}
    for f in &mut schema.downfills {fill(f,map,true)?;}
    for e in &mut schema.extractions {e.child=mapped(&e.child,map);}
    // Remaining table-bearing structural metadata is normalized through the
    // same typed codec; maintains' source/target are handled explicitly above.
    // These maps contain column identifiers, including arbitrary keys such as
    // "parent". Normalize their typed table slots, never their column strings.
    let mut extractions=std::mem::take(&mut schema.extractions);
    for e in &mut extractions {e.parent=mapped(&e.parent,map);}
    let columns=std::mem::take(&mut schema.asset_renames).into_iter().map(|(name,columns)|(mapped(&name,map),columns)).collect();
    schema.table_renames.clear();schema=remap(&schema,map)?;
    schema.extractions=extractions;schema.asset_renames=columns;
    Ok(schema)
}
fn fill(f:&mut crate::schema::backfill::Backfill,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>,down:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    *f=remap(f,map)?;
    f.program.name=format!("{}{}{}",if down{"down"}else{"backfill"},f.table,f.name);Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn prepare(conn:&Connection,next:&Schema,catalog:&mut BTreeMap<String,i64>)->Result<BTreeMap<String,String>,RuntimeError> {
    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_table_renames')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
    if !exists && next.table_renames.is_empty(){return Ok(BTreeMap::new())}
    conn.execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _table_renames(name TEXT PRIMARY KEY,origin TEXT NOT NULL) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    let mut names:BTreeMap<String,String>=conn.prepare("SELECT name,origin FROM _table_renames")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    for (new,old) in &next.table_renames {
        if new==old || !next.tables.contains_key(new) || next.tables.contains_key(old){return Err(refuse("table rename requires a distinct predecessor-only source"))}
        let origin=names.get(old).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||old.clone());
        if names.get(new).is_some_and(|root|root!=&origin){return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} conflicts with an independent retained identity")))}
        if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(catalog.get(old),catalog.get(new)) {if a!=b{return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} cannot merge existing table lifetimes")))}}
        for name in [old,new] {names.insert(name.clone(),origin.clone());conn.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _table_renames VALUES(?1,?2)",params![name,origin])?;}
    }
    let mut selected=BTreeMap::<String,String>::new();
    for name in next.tables.keys() {if let Some(root)=names.get(name) {if let Some(other)=selected.insert(root.clone(),name.clone()){return Err(refuse(format!("table aliases {other} and {name} cannot be active together")))}}}
    let map:BTreeMap<String,String>=names.iter().filter_map(|(name,root)|selected.get(root).filter(|new|*new!=name).map(|new|(name.clone(),new.clone()))).collect();
    for (old,new) in &map {
        if !catalog.contains_key(old){continue;}
        if catalog.contains_key(new){return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} conflicts with retained table storage")))}
        let entries:Vec<_>=catalog.iter().filter(|(key,_)|*key==old || key.starts_with(&format!("{old}."))).map(|(k,v)|(k.clone(),*v)).collect();
        for (key,num) in entries {let renamed=format!("{new}{}",&key[old.len()..]);conn.execute("UPDATE _catalog SET name=?1 WHERE name=?2",params![renamed,key])?;catalog.remove(&key);catalog.insert(renamed,num);}
        conn.execute("UPDATE _assets SET home_table=?1 WHERE home_table=?2",params![new,old])?;
        for table in ["_dormant_tables","_dormant_columns","_dormant_indexes"] {
            if conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {table} SET table_name=?1 WHERE table_name=?2"),params![new,old])?;}
        }
    }
    // Retained typed definitions must use the current public table names too.
    for table in ["_dormant_tables","_dormant_columns","_dormant_narrow_rows"] {
        if !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {continue;}
        let rows:Vec<(String,String)>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT definition,definition FROM {table} GROUP BY definition"))?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        for (old,text) in rows {let mut json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;identifiers(&mut json,&map);let new=canonical_json(&json);if old!=new {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {table} SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2"),params![new,old])?;}}
    }
    history(conn,&map)?;
    conn.execute("UPDATE _meta SET value=?1 WHERE key='version'",[VERSION])?;
    Ok(map)
}
fn history(conn:&Connection,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    for (table,down) in [("_backfill_definitions",false),("_downfill_definitions",true)] {
        if !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {continue;}
        let rows:Vec<(String,String)>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT digest,definition FROM {table}"))?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        for (old,text) in rows {let mut f:crate::schema::backfill::Backfill=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;fill(&mut f,map,down)?;let new=if down {f.down_key()}else{f.key()};if old==new{continue;}
            conn.execute(&format!("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO {table} VALUES(?1,?2)"),params![new,canonical_json(&f)])?;
            for ledger in ["_backfill_evidence","_backfill_pruned","_backfill_absence"] {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {ledger} SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2"),params![new,old])?;}
            conn.execute("UPDATE _backfill_absence SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2",params![format!("$native:{new}"),format!("$native:{old}")])?;
            conn.execute("UPDATE _backfill_absence SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2",params![format!("$authored:{new}"),format!("$authored:{old}")])?;
            for c in f.inputs.keys() {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_refs SET column_name=?1 WHERE column_name=?2",params![format!("$backfill:{new}:{c}"),format!("$backfill:{old}:{c}")])?;}
            conn.execute(&format!("DELETE FROM {table} WHERE digest=?1"),[old])?;
        }
    }Ok(())
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct IndexDecl {
    pub name: String,
    /// The columns, in order, the `words(..)` component left out.
    pub columns: Vec<String>,
    pub unique: bool,
    pub refusal: Option<(String, Option<String>)>,
    /// `words(a, b)`: its position among the components (the number of
    /// columns before it) and the text columns it tokenizes. An index with
    /// one serves `matching` reads whose keys are exactly those columns,
    /// and nothing else.
    pub words: Option<(usize, Vec<String>)>,
    pub computed: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, Expr>,
    pub site: Site,
}

impl IndexDecl {
    /// Whether the index serves ordinary prefix reads and probes.
    pub fn is_plain(&self) -> bool {
        self.words.is_none() && self.computed.is_empty()
    }

    /// Whether a `matching` read with this many keys reads this index.
    pub fn searches_after(&self, keys: usize) -> bool {
        self.words.as_ref().is_some_and(|(position, _)| *position == keys)
    }
}

//! Asset/reference renames transport cells, homes and lifetime evidence.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#8-wave-21-release-evolution — asset identity is preserved; no upload, re-claim or generated placeholder.
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
type Maps=BTreeMap<String,BTreeMap<String,String>>;
fn refusal(message:impl Into<String>)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Structural{code:"E_ADOPT",message:message.into()}}
fn replace_keys<T>(fields:BTreeMap<String,T>,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->BTreeMap<String,T> {fields.into_iter().map(|(name,value)|(map.get(&name).cloned().unwrap_or(name),value)).collect()}
fn table(table:&mut Table,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>){
    table.columns=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.columns),map);
    table.defaults=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.defaults),map);
    table.column_reads=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.column_reads),map);
    table.on_delete=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.on_delete),map);
    table.written_by=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.written_by),map);
    fn term(t:&mut crate::schema::RuleTerm,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>){use crate::schema::RuleTerm::*;if let Field(c)|OldField(c)|NextField(c)|Reference{column:c,..}|ReferenceChain{column:c,..}=t {if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}}}
    fn rule(r:&mut crate::schema::RuleExpr,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>){use crate::schema::RuleExpr::*;match r {Eq(a,b)|Ne(a,b)|Compare(_,a,b)=>{term(a,map);term(b,map)},And(xs)|Or(xs)=>for x in xs{rule(x,map)},Not(r)=>rule(r,map),Exists{key,equalities,..}|Created{key,equalities,..}=>{for t in key{term(t,map)}for(_,t)in equalities{term(t,map)}},_=>()}}
    for r in [&mut table.rules.read,&mut table.rules.insert,&mut table.rules.update,&mut table.rules.delete]{rule(r,map)}
    for r in table.column_reads.values_mut(){rule(r,map)}
    for index in table.indexes.values_mut(){for component in &mut index.components {if let crate::schema::IndexComponent::Column(c)=component {if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}}}}
    if let Some(sync)=&mut table.sync {
        for audience in std::iter::once(&mut sync.audience).chain(sync.also.iter_mut().map(|a|&mut a.audience)) {
            match audience {crate::schema::Audience::Column(c)=>{if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}},crate::schema::Audience::Target{prefix,..}=>for c in prefix {if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}},_=>()}
        }
    }
    for c in &mut table.constraints {match c{
        crate::schema::Constraint::Immutable{fields}|crate::schema::Constraint::Cap{by:fields,..}=>for f in fields{if let Some(new)=map.get(f){*f=new.clone();}},
        crate::schema::Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{key,..}=>for t in key{term(t,map)},
        _=>(),
    }}
}
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#20-visible-rollback-views-roll — comparison uses the same typed identity codec as activation.
impl Schema {
    /// Compare schema authority using declared physical column identities.
    pub fn with_column_names(&self,maps:&BTreeMap<String,BTreeMap<String,String>>)->Self{schema(self.clone(),maps)}
}
pub(super) fn schema(mut schema:Schema,maps:&Maps)->Schema {
    for(name,map)in maps {if let Some(t)=schema.tables.get_mut(name){table(t,map);}}
    for e in &mut schema.extractions {
        if let Some(map)=maps.get(&e.child){e.reference=map.get(&e.reference).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||e.reference.clone());e.fields=std::mem::take(&mut e.fields).into_iter().map(|(dest,source)|(map.get(&dest).cloned().unwrap_or(dest),source)).collect();}
        if let Some(map)=maps.get(&e.parent){for source in e.fields.values_mut(){if let Some(new)=map.get(source){*source=new.clone();}}}
    }schema
}
pub(super) fn prepare(conn:&Connection,next:&Schema,catalog:&BTreeMap<String,i64>,renamed_tables:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<Maps,RuntimeError>{
    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_asset_column_renames')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
    if !exists && next.asset_renames.is_empty(){return Ok(Maps::new())}
    conn.execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _asset_column_renames(t INTEGER NOT NULL,name TEXT NOT NULL,origin TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,name)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    let mut maps=Maps::new();
    for (name,current) in &next.tables {
        let Some(t)=catalog.get(name) else {continue};
        let mut aliases:BTreeMap<String,String>=conn.prepare("SELECT name,origin FROM _asset_column_renames WHERE t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        for (new,old) in next.asset_renames.get(name).into_iter().flatten() {
            if old==new || current.columns.contains_key(old) || !current.columns.get(new).is_some_and(asset){return Err(refusal(format!("asset rename {name}.{old} → {new} requires one predecessor-only source and one asset destination")))}
            let root=aliases.get(old).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||old.clone());
            if aliases.get(new).is_some_and(|r|r!=&root){return Err(refusal("asset rename cannot merge independent column lifetimes"))}
            for c in [old,new] {aliases.insert(c.clone(),root.clone());conn.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _asset_column_renames VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![t,c,root])?;}
        }
        let mut selected=BTreeMap::new();for c in current.columns.keys(){if let Some(root)=aliases.get(c){if selected.insert(root.clone(),c.clone()).is_some(){return Err(refusal("two names for the same asset column cannot be active together"))}}}
        let map:BTreeMap<_,_>=aliases.into_iter().filter_map(|(old,root)|selected.get(&root).filter(|new|**new!=old).map(|new|(old,new.clone()))).collect();
        for(old,new)in &map {
            let prior:Option<String>=conn.query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')",[],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
            if let Some(prior)=prior {
                let prior:Schema=serde_json::from_str(&prior).map_err(|e|refusal(e.to_string()))?;
                let prior=prior.with_table_names(renamed_tables)?;
                if let Some(before)=prior.table(name).filter(|t|t.columns.contains_key(old)){
                    let source=&before.columns[old];let destination=&current.columns[new];
                    if reference(source)||reference(destination){
                        if source!=destination||before.on_delete.get(old)!=current.on_delete.get(new)||before.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t|t.reference==*old)||current.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t|t.reference==*new){return Err(refusal("reference rename requires identical nominal type, deletion policy and uncaptured ownership in both directions"))}
                    }
                }
            }
            for rows in ["r","_dormant_rows","_dormant_narrow_rows"] {
                if !exists_table(conn,rows)?{continue;}
                let a=format!("$.Row.{}",serde_json::to_string(old).unwrap());let b=format!("$.Row.{}",serde_json::to_string(new).unwrap());
                let conflict:bool=conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM {rows} WHERE t=?1 AND json_type(data,?2) IS NOT NULL AND json_type(data,?3) IS NOT NULL AND json_extract(data,?2)!=json_extract(data,?3))"),params![t,a,b],|r|r.get(0))?;
                if conflict{return Err(refusal(format!("asset rename {name}.{old} → {new} has a conflicting physical destination")))}
                conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {rows} SET data=json_remove(json_set(data,?3,json_extract(data,?2)),?2) WHERE t=?1 AND json_type(data,?2) IS NOT NULL"),params![t,a,b])?;
            }
            conn.execute("UPDATE _assets SET home_column=?3 WHERE home_table=?1 AND home_column=?2",params![name,old,new])?;
            if exists_table(conn,"_dormant_refs")? {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_refs SET column_name=?3 WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2",params![t,old,new])?;}
            if exists_table(conn,"_dormant_values")? {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_values SET column_name=?3 WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2",params![t,old,new])?;}
            if exists_table(conn,"_dormant_columns")? {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_columns SET column_name=?3 WHERE table_name=?1 AND column_name=?2",params![name,old,new])?;}
        }
        if !map.is_empty(){
            for rows in ["_dormant_narrow_rows","_dormant_tables"] {
                if !exists_table(conn,rows)?{continue;}
                let condition=if rows=="_dormant_tables"{"table_name=?1"}else{"t=?1"};
                let value=if rows=="_dormant_tables"{rusqlite::types::Value::Text(name.clone())}else{rusqlite::types::Value::Integer(*t)};
                let definitions:Vec<String>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT DISTINCT definition FROM {rows} WHERE {condition}"))?.query_map([&value],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
                for text in definitions {let mut definition:Table=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;table(&mut definition,&map);conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {rows} SET definition=?2 WHERE {condition} AND definition=?3"),params![value,canonical_json(&definition),text])?;}
            }
            maps.insert(name.clone(),map);
        }
    }
    conn.execute("UPDATE _meta SET value=?1 WHERE key='version'",[super::table_renames::VERSION])?;
    Ok(maps)
}
fn exists_table(conn:&Connection,name:&str)->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{Ok(conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[name],|r|r.get(0))?)}
fn asset(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool {use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;match kind{Optional(k)|List{of:k,..}|ListRange{of:k,..}=>asset(k),File{..}|Image{..}|Video{..}|Ref{..}=>true,_=>false}}

fn reference(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool{use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;match kind{Optional(k)|List{of:k,..}|ListRange{of:k,..}=>reference(k),Ref{..}=>true,_=>false}}

//! Index choice and optional tuning. Examples compile in tests/guide.rs.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Indexes: chosen from the read

Indexes let reads seek the rows that match a predicate in the requested order.
The compiler chooses or creates them from your reads and declarations.

```quarry
table tasks:
  ownerId: principal
  projectId: id
  done: bool
  at: time
  unique ownerId, projectId else PROJECT_EXISTS 'This project already exists.'
  read <- .ownerId = viewer
  insert <- .ownerId = viewer
  update <- .ownerId = viewer
  online only
query one(projectId: id):
  return tasks[viewer, projectId]
query open(c: cursor ?):
  return tasks where .ownerId = viewer and .done = false first 20 by .at after c
```

`by .id` sorts ids, not creation times. `last N` reverses order: newest-first with chronological keys such as `.at`.

State the predicate and order. `check` chooses an existing index or creates
one. Rules, sync, caps and expiry also obtain their access indexes. A table
needs no `by` declaration for these reads.

`unique ownerId, projectId` is a product constraint: one row per pair.
`tasks[x]` selects identity when `x` is typed `id` or `tasks`; otherwise
exactly one declared unique key must accept the argument types in order.
Name columns when several keys accept those types: `tasks[ownerId = viewer,
projectId = projectId]`. Synthesized indexes never choose positional meaning.
In either key spelling (also `exists`), arguments for nullable columns of the
selected unique key must be provably non-null: use required types or `if x != null` guards
(`x != null and exists ...` in rules). Otherwise `E_QUARRY_NULL_KEY` refuses
the ambiguous null ties across replicas. Guard nullable arguments before
`require exists`: it proves row presence, not optional field presence.
To read null matches, use a bounded
`where` population ordered `by .id`, or address a row by id. Null for a
unique key argument returns no row at runtime, without scanning ties; the
guard is still required at compile time; maintained aggregate null groups are unchanged.
A fully pinned maintained group is unique even
with null, including rule probes that also test its value.
E_QUARRY_POINT_KEY refuses a point probe whose key can match several rows.
A fully pinned superset of a required unique key counts as unique too.
Use a unique key or a bounded `where` population; its scalar probe reports unavailable
when a scan is cut. Rule probes require a unique key.
A visible unique collision uses the optional `else CODE 'message'` hook;
a hidden collision keeps its ordinary authorization refusal. `if absent`
returns null on a visible clash. Codes are UPPER_SNAKE and never start with E_.

A bound row addresses itself: after `s = slots[slot] ! else NOT_FOUND`,
`slots[s]` reads, updates and deletes that row, as `slots[s.id]` does.

## Small lookup tables: `cap N`

A table that stays small (categories, plans, tags) declares `cap N` with no
`by`: at most N rows in the whole table. Every query may then read it whole,
filtered or not, with no `first N` and no cursor:

```quarry
table categories:
  name: text 1..40
  unique name else CATEGORY_EXISTS
  public 'shop categories'
  insert <- allow
  cap 50
  sync public
query categoryList():
  return categories by .name
mutation addCategory(name: text 1..40):
  return insert categories { name }
```

The 51st insert refuses `E_CONSTRAINT: categories holds at most 50 rows`.
A device predicts it when it holds the whole table (`sync public` with no
horizon); a private partition, a horizon or an expiring table queues the write for the server. A cap bounds a read only
up to the 8,192-row examined ceiling, and other meters still apply. Adding or
lowering a cap on a populated table checks the stored rows (`E_ADOPT`);
raising one applies in place. On an expiring table the cap counts live rows.
A table that can grow pages with `first N` and a cursor instead.

## Private lists: pin the audience

A private page is complete when its planned ranges decide the read rule.
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` prints the missing source edit for unsupported shapes.
The planner supplies party, membership and positive compound ranges, including
owner/public/member lists. An exact positive `where exists authors[.id, viewer]`
that repeats a read-rule disjunct supplies its own complete audience. Known
parent row images supply stored scalar pins; they do not prove copied child
fields equal their parents. A readable total static negative role probe may
also decide a range; hidden or lifetime-sensitive negative probes do not. A two-party row (buyer/seller, follower/followed, requester/owner) is
one query: under `.requester = viewer or .owner = viewer`, `swaps first 20 by
.id after c` (or `where .requester = viewer or .owner = viewer`) reads the
merge of the two pinned ranges, a row in both once, with one cursor, and is
complete. Each `or` branch must pin an index prefix that decides the rule, and
the predicate must be comparisons (no arithmetic or calls); up to 8 branches.
Pure membership and owner-or-membership work when the membership rule is an exact point:
`.owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]`. The usual `unique parent,
principal` needs no cap: it bounds witnesses per target row, not a viewer's
memberships. Discovery is metered and publishes no rows or cursor if cut. Add
`cap N by principal` only as a real enrollment limit; writes and adoption
enforce it, and bounded enrollment guarantees availability. The target can be
the row id (`exists members[.id, viewer]`) or a readable parent's id
(`exists offers[.offer, baker = viewer]`); page order and its id tie-breaker
remain. Otherwise the note prints one query per party. A child page pins the column its rule reads, read from the
parent it already names: `parent = cards[card] ! else NOT_FOUND`, then
`.board = parent.board`; copy that column onto the child as an immutable field
and check it in the insert rule. A capacity check reads a count whose read rule
covers every writer (it discloses the count, not the rows), then `require`s
below the limit; a check over the private rows themselves is E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT.

```quarry
table swaps:
  requester: principal
  owner: principal
  item: text <= 80
  read <- .requester = viewer or .owner = viewer
  insert <- .requester = viewer
  online only
query mySwaps(c: cursor ?):
  return swaps first 20 by .id after c

table boards:
  owner: principal
  read <- exists boardMembers[.id, viewer]
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table boardMembers:
  board: boards
  principal: principal
  immutable board, principal
  unique board, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  insert <- created boards[.board]
  online only
table cards:
  board: boards
  title: text <= 80
  immutable board
  read <- exists boardMembers[.board, viewer]
  insert <- exists boardMembers[.board, viewer]
  online only
table comments:
  board: boards
  card: cards
  body: text <= 500
  immutable board, card
  read <- exists boardMembers[.board, viewer]
  insert <- exists boardMembers[.board, viewer] and exists cards[.card, board = .board]
  online only
query cardComments(card: cards, c: cursor ?):
  parent = cards[card] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return comments where .board = parent.board and .card = card first 20 by .id after c

table shifts:
  coordinator: principal
  slots: int
  public 'shift times and capacity are public'
  insert <- .coordinator = viewer
  online only
table signups:
  shift: shifts
  volunteer: principal
  immutable shift, volunteer
  unique shift, volunteer else ALREADY_SIGNED_UP
  read <- .volunteer = viewer
  insert <- .volunteer = viewer
  online only
view signupCount: count signups by shift
  read <- allow
mutation signUp(shift: shifts):
  s = shifts[shift] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require signupCount[shift] < s.slots else SHIFT_FULL 'This shift is full'
  return insert signups { shift, volunteer: viewer }
```

A page may combine a party with a static role (`exists roles[principal =
viewer, role = 'admin']`), including a synced union. The server pages all rows
for a readable grant holder and only party rows for everyone else. The device
walks its acquired union rows without probing a remote role. Explicit pages
need no whole-table cap; an omitted page bound must still have a cap or total
unique over its original prefix. An owner cap never bounds a staff's whole table.

Positive compound rules such as `.owner = viewer or (exists staff[viewer]
and .status = 'open')` keep the status pin in the staff range. Parent membership
and status alternatives work the same way when the parent source is readable.
A nullable enum's `.species != null` enumerates its non-null variants. At most
seven alternatives plus the system range; arithmetic and negated probes keep
an exact diagnostic repair. Discovery still must complete before returning rows.

An unreadable parent-owner source cannot prove absence through an index or
cap. For an immutable parent owner, copy it onto the child as an immutable
principal, check that copy in the insert rule, and fill every insert and seed.
`check` prints the actual table/column declarations and payload edit. Existing
rows need a backfill before adoption; mutable parent owners need transactional
copy propagation. Keep the parent private.

A grant that reads no row field, such as `exists staff[viewer]` or
`viewer = 'service:ops'`, admits every row to its holder. When it holds for
the caller, and the caller may read the row that grants it (`read <-
.principal = viewer` on `staff`), the page is decided once: a staff page is
`complete`. When the rule's other disjuncts are parties (`.owner = viewer or
exists staff[viewer]`), everyone else's page is their party range, complete
too: one query serves both.
A grant row its holder cannot read decides nothing; the note says when a
grant is not used. The run never needs a guard, but `check` follows one: a
page that only grant holders reach (`require exists staff[viewer] else …`,
`if exists staff[viewer]:` around it, or `if not exists staff[viewer]: return
[]` before it; several grants may share one guard with `or`) gets no note, as
it is `complete` for every caller who runs it. A disjunct whose row columns
the scan pins (`exists rides[.ride, leader = viewer]` under `.ride = ride`)
decides the page the same way, with the pinned values, when its holders may
read the row it probes: the leader's roster is `complete`, and `check`
follows `if` or `require` of `exists rides[ride, leader = viewer]`.

A page that stays `complete: false` still holds every row the caller may
read. In `test.cases.jsonl` the default expectation passes it (write
`"expect": "capped"` to pin it); a client renders the rows and shows
`readPresentation(read).incomplete` as "there may be more", not as an error.

## Operations

Run `snapback4 check --cost` for chosen and synthesized indexes and the reads
that need them. `snapback4 why <operation>` shows each source site, selected
index, key bindings and result form. These are structural facts; use the dev
read meter to measure actual work (`guide reads`).
Index mismatch and ambiguity repairs print source only after the edit compiles
in your project; otherwise the diagnostic gives prose guidance.
The default output names planner indexes by their columns. `--verbose` and
`--json` retain their canonical names; authored index names stay as written.
Two-column labels keep a trailing `id`, as in `index (org, id)`; wider labels
may omit the final `id` tiebreaker.

Equality pins lead the index, then any time bucket, then the requested order.
`id` breaks ties in the same direction. Identical index definitions are reused.
A nullable unique key permits tied null rows, so its columns alone cannot
carry a page cursor: the planner uses a separate ordering with `id`.
Generated names are stable across declaration order. Adding a query can build
an index on adoption, but cannot change another positional key's meaning.

A finite time window ordered by another column uses engine-chosen buckets.
Write both bounds, such as `.at > now - 1d and .at <= now`; exact endpoint
filters still run after authorization. A missing bound remains a residual
scan. `guide feeds` shows the complete Trending query.

A `search body, caption` line declares searchable columns; `matching q`
chooses a words index for the predicate and order (`guide search`).
A unique key can group time without adding a stored column:

```quarry
table checkins:
  principal: principal
  at: time
  unique principal, day(at) else ALREADY_CHECKED_IN 'One check-in per day.'
  read <- .principal = viewer
  insert <- .principal = viewer
mutation checkin():
  return insert checkins { principal: viewer, at: now }
```

`day`, `hour` and `bucket` use floor ordinals from the UTC epoch. An optional
offset anchors the boundary; `day(at, 8h)` starts at 08:00 UTC. These are index
keys, absent from row shapes, rules and writable columns. Changing a period
or offset rebuilds the index. A sweep may order by a computed key only when
its underlying columns are immutable.

Existing `by name: columns` table lines remain optional tuning. Reuse requires
an identical ordered definition. The language card's final block shows the
older explicit read syntax; ordinary reads continue to name columns.

## Refusals

E_CONSTRAINT means an intrinsic id unique key, product cap, immutable field, format or
postcondition rejected the write. Keep the rule; correct its input.
For a visible duplicate handle, choose another; `if absent` is
appropriate only when doing nothing on an existing row is the intended write.


E_INDEX_KEY_BYTES means an encoded key exceeds 2048 bytes, including its
components and row id. Bound the named text column, for example `text <=80`,
and shorten or delete old oversized values before adoption. Index checks
allow up to four UTF-8 bytes per character. Table and index names together
must fit 512 UTF-8 bytes. Deletes remain available; updates may retain or
shorten existing oversized keys without growing their lengths or count.
Both server stores and devices enforce new keys; search tokens have their own cap.
For a generated index, the diagnostic names the read or declaration's source
site and the column to bound. Change that column's type; no generated index
declaration needs editing.

E_CURSOR_INDEX concerns an older explicit index that lacks a total order.
For new reads, use `tasks where .ownerId = viewer first 20 by .at after c`:
the planner adds the id tiebreak. For a non-unique explicit index, append `id`
to its definition or apply the diagnostic's mechanical `check --fix`.
Keep nullable unique constraints unchanged; use the column-ordered read
above to obtain a separate page index without weakening uniqueness.

E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN refuses a positional key that cannot identify one
declared unique key by argument types. E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS refuses several matching
indexes. Name columns to state the key; if indexes share those columns in
different orders, choose an explicit index. Offered index repairs compile in
context; `check --fix` can apply a verified positional choice.

E_SCHEMA_CONSTRAINT means the named declaration violates a schema constraint.
Keep a product cap such as `cap 50 by conversationId` when it is a real rule;
the compiler supplies its index. For an audience mismatch, match `sync` to
the read rule rather than exposing private rows. Rerun `snapback4 check`."##;
# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```
For Postgres, prefer `SNAPBACK4_STORE_URL` with explicit schema/ha=none and Unix sockets; a password in `--store-url` is visible in process arguments. Serve requires `--worker-state-dir /absolute/durable/path` or `SNAPBACK4_WORKER_STATE_DIR`. Queries, identified writes, sessions, ephemeral state and ordinary maintained count/sum columns are admitted. A deploy may add, redefine or drop those columns and indexes on them; it atomically recounts sources and mirrors parents before publishing the generation. Ordinary releases may also add tables, add optional or literal-defaulted columns, add/drop/redefine indexes, and retire ordinary tables or columns into dormant storage. Use `deploy DIR --restore-dormant` to restore retired names and their exact rows/values. A restored column must repeat its retained declaration: changing its type, default, text constraints or deletion policy refuses just as an in-place edit would. Unique collisions refuse the whole activation. Ordinary Postgres schema evolution currently refuses `evolution-authority` when the schema contains a durable temporal, read-through or recorded table, including an unchanged one. Their activation-time source retirement needs the later lifetime slice. Previously admitted maintained-column transitions with unchanged ordinary declarations remain available. Existing column types/defaults, rules, audiences and other authority declarations must stay unchanged. Type narrowing, backfills, renames, temporal/read-through/recorded evolution and retirement of tables with maintained columns remain named refusals. Token minting (`token`), SQL/data and release inspection, rollback, previews, prune, staging, saved versions and hot activation refuse; excluded runners, certified sync and complete configuration: `guide release-json`. SDK guest/signup/login and proof seedAccounts retry explicit identity 429 refusals using retryAfterMs (Retry-After for older servers). Sign-in has at most four attempts within timeoutMs (default three minutes); signal cancels requests/waits. No lost or malformed reply is replayed. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development and classified staging proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`); production always uses wall time.

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity (SQLite)
Add `use identity password` (below). Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```
Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness. Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat (SQLite)
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```
```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```
This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads. A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption (SQLite)
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or a declarative `backfill table name(fields: types): {column: expression}`; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility. Generations climb on actual switches, including rollback/forward; identical execution content is a no-op.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune (SQLite)
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused deploy removes its candidate directory and private stage. An ambiguous reply preserves source until committed history resolves the outcome; inspect releases before retrying. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Source identical to the active release is a no-op; source matching a retained release reactivates its existing id with restoration and full validation. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. Deploy/rollback wait up to five seconds for a shutting-down store owner before admission; live serve keeps its authenticated control path. A remaining owner refuses with E_STORE_BUSY; stop that owned process before retrying. Failed or ambiguous activation is never automatically replayed. `await host.stop()` from startServe waits for its native child and the kernel store lock, including delayed worker cleanup, before resolving. `serve ./deployment --wait-stopped` is the same bounded five-second lock barrier for a retained process handle after signalling/reaping it; it neither stops a process nor opens SQLite. It refuses other serve flags. A timeout rejects cleanup rather than claiming the store is free.
Dev admits loopback personas or bearer sessions for queries, writes and the compatibility record; serve requires bearer sessions even for public rows. Identity config/sign-in endpoints are public. Seed/dev endpoints refuse in serve; system job doors require full service seats. `guide wire` gives the HTTP contract. Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; selecting retained source by deploy or rollback already selects restoration. The same --restore-dormant flag is accepted by both commands. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Visible rollback by default (SQLite)
Deploy prepares compiled rollback views when a predecessor cannot hold newly allowed values. With current independent intent, changed source also prepares a view without widening, so a repaired workflow cannot silently roll back to its unsafe old body. These views retain the complete authored current schema, helpers, programs and policy while admitting old names and inputs; unsupported mappings require authored compatible guarded source. Old inputs may fit wider same-format text/list bounds or omit new optional arguments; narrowed or removed inputs still refuse. `rollback --to 1` selects release 1's registered view in the active deployment's context, retaining current guards and widened domains; output names both original and actual ids. The original source remains immutable. After upgrading the CLI/runtime, restart an older running serve before switching releases; an older host refuses the new custody control feature before admission. `--exact` deliberately selects that source and reports its dormant population. Newer views never become ordinary latest. If a guard cannot be transported safely or a view cannot compile, deploy refuses until source supplies a compatible workflow. Nullable old results remain a client compatibility change: render an amount still to follow as pending, never zero. Optional media must remain optional in old UI consumers.

Intentional null hiding uses `rollback 1 hides orders where fee = null because 'An unquoted fee must not look free.'` in candidate source. The writer permits only those null rows and their actual hidden-reference dependents. Widened nonnull values still stay visible. Removed defaulted fields require truthful `down` expressions; previews suggest cross-table candidates for moved values, never silently show a misleading old default. Snapshot previews do not freeze later writes: activation revalidates under its writer transaction.

## Bridge a safety gate before exact rollback (SQLite)
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting exact v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees (SQLite)
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

These dormant-row outcomes require `rollback <deployment> --to 1 --exact`. A registered default view keeps widened old-table rows visible with the current guard. Ordinary renames, required-field additions and splits with a fitting down do not require a view merely because the app declares an invariant. A needed view uses the current workflow or a compatible loosened bridge with current guards. A guarded current view names removed nullable fields as absent results and removed writes as E_OP; author a compatible current endpoint to keep an old write available. On a pre-0.4.39 store, a refusal names `rollback <deployment> --to N --exact` and "redeploy with 0.4.39 to register a view"; preview exact selection with --check before accepting its historical behavior. Absent operations still return E_OP. Test audiences and guards with real accounts.

Rollback previews lead with records hidden from tables the target release has, then empty/defaulted legacy cells and newer-only table rows. Counts precede read rules; they do not infer birth release or a viewer’s query result. Declare a reverse projection for a truthful old representation, or keep a bridge. Guard/refusal and query/count/safety diffs follow. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Predicate-only read-through changes preserve bound captures and parent lifetimes; structural capture changes and new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback (SQLite)
Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. Required operation parameters remain required; only declared optional, defaulted or deprecated arguments may be omitted. Baseline generation also warns about old-client input breaks and result/refusal changes. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.

`useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/existing-argument signatures differ or the server adds a required argument; extra optional served parameters are compatible. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.

## Trigger a deployed job (SQLite)
A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
```sh
export SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
bunx snapback4 run reconcile --release ./deployment --json
unset SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN
```
`--token-env NAME` reads another environment variable. The command uses served source, so it also works outside the app directory. A lost reply is ambiguous; inspect the queue before retrying. Starter/client recipes: `guide client`.

`guide production` covers real accounts: add signup/login and persistent sessions, replace personas with principals, provision authorized profiles/roles and memberships, wait for scopes before offline reads, and bootstrap without fixtures. Prove role checks, sign-out, restart and rollback on durable storage behind TLS (`guide client`).

## Inspection, proxies and TLS (SQLite)

`data TABLE --release ./deployment --json` returns `{rows, more, limit}`. The default limit is 50; `more:true` means the snapshot was truncated. Increase `--limit N` to inspect more; `--limit 0` returns no rows and reports whether any exist. Non-JSON output remains one row per line. `snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json` returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. `sql --dormant --release ./deployment` shows retained rows/cells, including removed tables; `sql --all-releases --release ./deployment` adds active images. `_snapback_state`, `_snapback_source` and `_snapback_columns` identify provenance and provided cells; these are retained images, not historical snapshots. Both modes are read-only; internal tables remain unavailable. Inspection bypasses read rules and can show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface. Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS. Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity. `--json-objects` returns `{header, columns, rows, limit, more, ruleFiltered:false}` with object `rows`; `--json` uses positional `rows`. Put flags outside the quoted SQL. N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.

## Capture a saved version (SQLite)
After check and business cases pass, from the app root:
```sh
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --save-version versions/v1
snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check --bridge ./bridge
snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --restore-dormant --check
```
The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete target Quarry operations with compatible value domains loosened to include current data, retaining new tables with their current rules, plus this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.

## Keep v1 clients working (SQLite)

`deploy --check --json` reports `clientCompatibility` separately from data adoption: missing operations, changed kinds, required new arguments, removed or narrowed inputs, result shape/value changes and changed refusal codes/messages. Deploy prints the same warnings. Input breaks name the old release and the E_INPUT/E_OP outcome. A result or behavior review asks you to verify the intended contract; a correct new privacy denial is intentional behavior, not an input shape failure. The comparison is conservative, not a proof of unchanged logic. For example, save this v1 source and its callers sending `{name:"Ada",obsolete:"old input"}` to `greet`:

```quarry
use identity guests
query greet(name: text <=40, obsolete: text <=40):
  return {message: name + '!'}
```
Deploy this v2 source. The unchanged v1 call still returns `{message:"Ada!"}`; new callers can use `greeting` and omit `suffix` and `obsolete`:

```quarry
use identity guests
query greeting(displayName: text <=40 alias name, suffix: text <=1 default '!', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated):
  return {message: displayName + suffix}
alias greet = greeting
```
`alias name` maps the old argument to `displayName`; supply either spelling, never both. Use the alias for renames; `deprecated` deliberately discards its value. A literal default handles omitted or null arguments and must fit the declared scalar type; explicit non-null input still validates. `deprecated` accepts a validated optional argument but forbids referring to it in the body. Unknown arguments still refuse. These modifiers apply to non-native Quarry queries and mutations; helpers, actions and jobs keep their distinct invocation contracts. Aliases target an authored query/mutation in the same module, carry the same rules/guards/results, and cannot chain. Retain the old endpoint until its callers have retired; preserve the old result projection and handle intended denials.

Deploy registers a compiled visible view for widened domains and relaxed unique keys, or refuses unsupported adaptation. It also reports remaining reverse-projection risks: dropping a required old column or widening a value domain can strand newly written rows. The preflight separately attempts exact reverse adoption on its post-deploy snapshot, naming present-data failures. Keep old fields and compatible domains in an expand/contract bridge; after writes run the named `rollback <deployment> --to N --check --bridge <new-dir>` before selecting N. A green snapshot never freezes later writes.

## Persistent production: SQLite v1 → v2 → rollback → forward

Replacement transforms read reversible preimages; input types cover original/native rows. `down table name(typedInputs): {oldField: expression}` declares a reversible old view for newer rows; exact old preimages win, conflicting projected-cell edits refuse forward. Native preimages and old-view outputs must be authoritative scalars; use separate scalar columns instead of replacing a reference, asset, collection or lifetime field. Reference inputs retain their parent lifetimes across older views; deleted/reused parents refuse forward. Unrepresentable values stay dormant. `newField: Type renamed from oldField` carries a scalar cell both ways; asset columns and asset lists move their actual cell and single home together. Reference renames require identical target/type/on-delete semantics and retain lifetime evidence; engine-owned moves refuse before activation; keep old operation/result wrappers where clients need them.

A table declares `table observations renamed from behaviors:` to preserve its row/reference identity and old-release writes; it refuses merging independent table histories. Ordinary backfill expressions may read indexed cross-table `exists` or unique points against one pre-patch snapshot. The language-card example uses migration-only `fileText(file)` with `nonemptyLines(text)` for repeated nonempty UTF-8 entries; unavailable bytes, invalid UTF-8 or a job-meter cut refuse atomically. It never supplies ordinary mutation-time file validation. Active writes refresh accepted backfill inputs; older-release edits still need recomputation agreement or conflict repair. An absent optional rename destination is not a conflict.

Computed `down` cannot replace files/images, asset lists, references or a missing table. Required → optional media, an album becoming empty, or nullable money needs a generated loosened-target bridge when no truthful scalar substitution exists. Review target operations reading loosened columns and nullable UI assumptions. Keep current safety guards absent from the target; retaining new tables does not make them visible through frozen old operations. Automatic registered views handle compatible widenings. For an authored bridge, deploy it as a new release with `--restore-dormant`, first with `--check`.

Save this script and run it with Node. `PRODUCTION_DIR` selects its persistent store; reruns preserve rows/users. It proves backfill, privacy and rollback/forward behavior. This example widens the stored weight domain, so deploy registers a view. Default rollback preserves current privacy through that view; `--exact` deliberately restores v1's broader reads after an explicit review. REL5 owns reactivation/old-client reporting; SEC3 owns privacy/audit.

```js
// day-two-production.mjs — this exact fence runs against the packed package.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve } from "node:path";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { cli, deploy, rollback, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
import { signup } from "snapback4/auth";
const directory = resolve(process.env.PRODUCTION_DIR ?? "production-day-two");
const v1 = `use identity password
table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('member', 'reviewer')
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('member', 'reviewer')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] {principal, role}
table tickets:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  legacy: text <=200 ?
  weight: int 0..5000
  length: int
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'reviewer']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation add(body: text 1..200, weight: int 0..5000, length: int):
  return insert tickets {owner:viewer, body, legacy:body, weight, length}
mutation revise(ticket: tickets, weight: int 0..5000):
  update tickets[ticket] {weight}
query ticket(ticket: tickets):
  return tickets[ticket]
`;
const v2 = v1.replaceAll("weight: int 0..5000", "weight: int 0..10000").replace("  owner: principal", "  vehicleClass: enum('car', 'van', 'commercial')\n  owner: principal")
  .replace("  read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[principal = viewer, role = 'reviewer']", "  read <- .owner = viewer")
  .replace("{owner:viewer, body, legacy:body, weight, length}", "{owner:viewer, body, legacy:body, weight, length, vehicleClass:'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car'}")
  + `backfill tickets vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  {vehicleClass:'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car'}
-- Spec: only the ticket owner may revise its weight.
invariant tickets.revise only .owner = viewer
invariant tickets.read only .owner = viewer
down tickets legacyText(body: text 1..200): {legacy:body}
`;
for (const [version, source] of [["v1", v1], ["v2", v2]]) {
  mkdirSync(`versions/${version}`, { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(`versions/${version}/schema.q`, source);
}
async function checked(args) {
  const result = await cli(args);
  assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr + result.stdout);
  return result.stdout;
}
async function snapshot() {
  const count = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tickets", "--release", directory, "--json"])).rows[0][0];
  const full = JSON.parse(await checked(["sql", "SELECT id, owner, body, weight, length FROM tickets ORDER BY id", "--release", directory, "--limit", String(Math.max(1, count + 1)), "--json"]));
  assert.equal(full.rows.length, count, "complete population");
  return new Map(full.rows.map(row => [row[0], row]));
}
if (existsSync(directory + "/store.sqlite")) await rollback(directory, 1, {args:["--exact"]});
else await deploy(directory, "versions/v1");
const host = await startServe(directory);
try {
  const before = await snapshot();
  const users = [];
  for (const role of ["member", "reviewer"]) {
    const signed = await signup(host.url, `${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, randomUUID() + randomUUID());
    assert.equal(signed.ok, true);
    users.push(signed.session);
  }
  const [alice, reviewer] = users;
  async function post(path, session, args) {
    const response = await fetch(host.url + path, { method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json", authorization:`Bearer ${session.token}`}, body:JSON.stringify({args}), signal:AbortSignal.timeout(10000) });
    assert.equal(response.status, 200);
    return response.json();
  }
  const operator = {token:await host.operatorToken()};
  for (const [index, role] of ["member", "reviewer"].entries()) {
    assert.equal((await post("/m/provision", operator, {principal:users[index].principal, role})).state, "sent");
  }
  const first = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"historical ticket", weight:1000, length:400});
  assert.equal(first.state, "sent");
  const ticket = first.result.id;
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data.body, "historical ticket", "v1 reviewer can read");
  const historical = await snapshot(); assert.equal(historical.size, before.size + 1);
  const preview = await checked(["deploy", directory, "--schema", "versions/v2", "--check", "--restore-dormant"]);
  assert.match(preview, /vehicleClass/);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "read-only preview");
  await deploy(directory, "versions/v2", {args:["--restore-dormant"]});
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), historical, "historical fields preserved");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "car");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "v2 tightened privacy");
  const oldCall = await post("/m/add", alice, {body:"old-shaped call on v2", weight:4000, length:400});
  assert.equal(oldCall.state, "sent"); assert.equal(oldCall.result.vehicleClass, "commercial");
  const allV2 = await snapshot(); assert.equal(allV2.size, historical.size + 1);
  await rollback(directory, 1);
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), allV2, "native rows preserved"); assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "default view keeps current privacy");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "car");
  await rollback(directory, 1, {args:["--exact"]}); // Deliberate original-source exercise; default keeps v2 policy.
  const oldView = (await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data;
  assert.equal(oldView.body, "historical ticket"); assert.ok(!("vehicleClass" in oldView));
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket:oldCall.result.id})).data.legacy, "old-shaped call on v2", "declared reverse view for native row");
  assert.equal((await post("/m/revise", alice, {ticket, weight:4000})).state, "sent");
  const changed = await snapshot(); const expected = new Map(allV2); const changedRow = [...expected.get(ticket)]; changedRow[3] = 4000; expected.set(ticket, changedRow);
  assert.deepEqual(changed, expected, "only intended edit");
  await rollback(directory, "latest");
  assert.deepEqual(await snapshot(), changed, "complete population preserved");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", alice, {ticket})).data.vehicleClass, "commercial", "old edit caught up");
  assert.equal((await post("/q/ticket", reviewer, {ticket})).data, null, "privacy restored");
  console.log(JSON.stringify({status:"passed", persistent:true, before:before.size, after:changed.size, backfill:"car → commercial after v1 edit", privacy:"v1 broad → v2 private → default private → exact v1 broad → v2 private"}));
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```
Storage, users and rows survive. Resolve reported conflicts through a truthful bridge.
Deploy and deploy --check lead with a verdict and test status. Changed-scope security review is last, followed by a repeated verdict; full JSON retains all findings. Ordinary check policy belongs to SEC7. The recorded test result includes its age; stale or missing evidence is explicit. Without a matching completed campaign they demand `snapback4 test` for the selected source and fixtures (saved-source previews name `test --source`). Add `--strict` to refuse reachable refuted intent before activation; unproved intent and no-invariant notes remain advisory. A finite campaign is evidence, not proof. See `guide security` for the independent-intent loop.

Migration warnings require a recorded older version crossing the behavior's introduction. Fresh init/check stays quiet; stderr delivery acknowledges the installed version, including CI. Paths cover session literals, advance arithmetic, persistent startDev and data --json. Future known-family store formats refuse with “a newer Snapback (FORMAT)”; upgrade the CLI. SQL inspection also exits nonzero.

//! Compare final-existence obligations in declared row/column identity space.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#20-visible-rollback-views-roll — spelling-only changes must not trigger a view.
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Constraint, RuleTerm, Schema};
use crate::parser;

#[derive(Default)]
struct Identities {
    tables: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    columns: BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>,
}
impl Identities {
    fn table(&self, name: &str) -> String {self.tables.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||name.into())}
    fn column(&self, table: &str, name: &str) -> String {
        self.columns.get(table).and_then(|c|c.get(name)).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||name.into())
    }
    fn term(&self, source: &str, term: &RuleTerm) -> RuleTerm {
        match term {
            RuleTerm::Field(c)=>RuleTerm::Field(self.column(source,c)),
            RuleTerm::OldField(c)=>RuleTerm::OldField(self.column(source,c)),
            RuleTerm::NextField(c)=>RuleTerm::NextField(self.column(source,c)),
            RuleTerm::Reference{table,column,field,old}=>RuleTerm::Reference{
                table:self.table(table),column:self.column(source,column),field:self.column(table,field),old:*old},
            RuleTerm::ReferenceChain{column,hops,old}=>RuleTerm::ReferenceChain{
                column:self.column(source,column),old:*old,hops:hops.iter().map(|h|snapback4_core::schema::RuleHop{
                    table:self.table(&h.table),field:self.column(&h.table,&h.field)}).collect()},
            RuleTerm::Viewer|RuleTerm::Literal(_)=>term.clone(),
        }
    }
    fn signature(&self,schema:&Schema,source:&str,constraint:&Constraint)->Option<(String,Vec<String>,Vec<RuleTerm>)> {
        let Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{table,index,key}=constraint else{return None};
        let index=schema.table(table)?.index(index)?;
        if !index.is_plain(){return None;}
        Some((self.table(table),index.columns().iter().map(|c|self.column(table,c)).collect(),
            key.iter().map(|t|self.term(source,t)).collect()))
    }
}

pub(super) fn relaxed(target:&[(String,String)],current:&[(String,String)])->bool {
    let old=target.iter().flat_map(|(f,t)|parser::parse_document(f,t).0.tables).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if !old.iter().any(|t|!t.final_exists.is_empty()){return false;}
    let next=current.iter().flat_map(|(f,t)|parser::parse_document(f,t).0.tables).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let (before,after)=(crate::compile(target),crate::compile(current));
    if !before.diagnostics.is_empty()||!after.diagnostics.is_empty(){return true;}
    let mut identities=Identities::default();
    let mut pairs=Vec::new();
    for prior in &old {
        let Some(now)=next.iter().find(|n|n.name==prior.name||n.renamed_from.as_ref()==Some(&prior.name)||prior.renamed_from.as_ref()==Some(&n.name))else{continue};
        identities.tables.insert(now.name.clone(),prior.name.clone());
        let mut columns=BTreeMap::new();
        for c in &prior.columns {
            if let Some(n)=now.columns.iter().find(|n|n.name==c.name||n.renamed_from.as_ref()==Some(&c.name)||c.renamed_from.as_ref()==Some(&n.name)) {
                columns.insert(n.name.clone(),c.name.clone());
            }
        }
        if now.columns.iter().map(|c|columns.get(&c.name).unwrap_or(&c.name)).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>().len()!=now.columns.len(){return true;}
        identities.columns.insert(now.name.clone(),columns);
        pairs.push((prior.name.as_str(),now.name.as_str()));
    }
    // Never let two independent current identities collapse to one old name.
    if next.iter().map(|t|identities.table(&t.name)).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>().len()!=next.len(){return true;}
    let unchanged=Identities::default();
    for (prior,now) in pairs {
        let (Some(old_table),Some(new_table))=(before.schema.table(prior),after.schema.table(now))else{return true};
        for constraint in &old_table.constraints {
            if !matches!(constraint,Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{..}){continue;}
            let Some(expected)=unchanged.signature(&before.schema,prior,constraint)else{return true};
            if !new_table.constraints.iter().any(|c|identities.signature(&after.schema,now,c).as_ref()==Some(&expected)){return true;}
        }
    }
    false
}
